U.S. state

This article is about the U.S. state. For other uses, see [Indiana (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_(disambiguation) "Indiana (disambiguation)").

"Hoosier State" redirects here. For the passenger train, see [*Hoosier State* (train)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoosier_State_(train) "Hoosier State (train)").

| [Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_minerals,_rocks,_stones_and_gemstones "List of U.S. state minerals, rocks, stones and gemstones") | [Indiana limestone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_limestone "Indiana limestone")[[13]](#cite_note-State_River_and_Stone-14) |
| Other | [Wabash River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wabash_River "Wabash River") (state river)[[13]](#cite_note-State_River_and_Stone-14) [Republic P-47 Thunderbolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_P-47_Thunderbolt "Republic P-47 Thunderbolt") *Hoosier Spirit II* (state aircraft)[[14]](#cite_note-15) |
| [State route marker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Numbered_highways_in_the_United_States#State_highways "Numbered highways in the United States") |

| [Lists of United States state symbols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lists_of_United_States_state_symbols "Lists of United States state symbols") |

**Indiana** ([/ˌɪndiˈænə/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [ⓘ](/wiki/File:En-us-Indiana.ogg "File:En-us-Indiana.ogg") [*IN-dee-AN-ə*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"))[[15]](#cite_note-16) is a [state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._state "U.S. state") in the [Midwestern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States "Midwestern United States") region of the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States "United States"). It borders [Lake Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Michigan "Lake Michigan") to the northwest, [Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan "Michigan") to the north and northeast, [Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio "Ohio") to the east, the [Ohio River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_River "Ohio River") and [Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kentucky "Kentucky") to the south and southeast, and the [Wabash River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wabash_River "Wabash River") and [Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois "Illinois") to the west. Nicknamed "the [Hoosier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoosier "Hoosier") State",[[16]](#cite_note-17) Indiana is the [38th-largest by area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_area "List of U.S. states and territories by area") and the [17th-most populous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_population "List of U.S. states and territories by population") of the [50 states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_territories_of_the_United_States "List of states and territories of the United States"). The state's capital and largest city is [Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis "Indianapolis"). Indiana was admitted to the Union as the 19th state on December 11, 1816.

[Indigenous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas "Indigenous peoples of the Americas") resistance to American settlement was broken with their defeat in [Battle of Tippecanoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Tippecanoe "Battle of Tippecanoe") in 1811, and the collapse of [Tecumseh's confederacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tecumseh's_confederacy "Tecumseh's confederacy") in 1813. The new settlers were primarily Americans of [British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_people "British people") ancestry from the [eastern seaboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Coast_of_the_United_States "East Coast of the United States") and the [Upland South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upland_South "Upland South"), and [Germans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germans "Germans"). After the [Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War "American Civil War"), in which the state fought for the [Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_(American_Civil_War) "Union (American Civil War)"), natural gas attracted heavy industry and new [European immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_immigration_to_the_United_States "European immigration to the United States") to its northern counties. In the first half of the 20th century, northern and central sections experienced a boom in goods manufacture and automobile production. Southern Indiana remained largely rural. After the rise and fall of the [Klan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Klan "Indiana Klan") in the 1920s, the state swung politically from the [Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_(United_States) "Republican Party (United States)") to [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_(United_States) "Democratic Party (United States)") in the [New Deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Deal "New Deal") 1930s. Today, with a decades-long record of returning Republican majorities, Indiana is counted a "[red state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_states_and_blue_states "Red states and blue states")".

Indiana has [a diverse economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Indiana "Economy of Indiana") with a gross state product in 2023 of 404.3 billion.[[17]](#cite_note-18) Indianapolis is at the center of the state's largest [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_metropolitan_area "Indianapolis metropolitan area"), with a population of over two million.[[18]](#cite_note-19) The [Fort Wayne metro area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Wayne_metropolitan_area,_Indiana "Fort Wayne metropolitan area, Indiana") follows with a population of 645,000.[[19]](#cite_note-2010census-20)

The state's largest city, [Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis "Indianapolis"), is home to professional sports teams, including the [NFL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Football_League "National Football League")'s [Indianapolis Colts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Colts "Indianapolis Colts"), the [NBA's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Basketball_Association "National Basketball Association") [Indiana Pacers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Pacers "Indiana Pacers"), and the [WNBA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_National_Basketball_Association "Women's National Basketball Association")'s [Indiana Fever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Fever "Indiana Fever"). The city also hosts several notable competitive events, such as the [Indianapolis 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_500 "Indianapolis 500"), held at [Indianapolis Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Motor_Speedway "Indianapolis Motor Speedway").

## Etymology

Indiana's name means "Land of the [Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States "Native Americans in the United States")", or simply "Indian Land".[[b]](#cite_note-21) It also stems from Indiana's territorial history. On May 7, 1800, the [United States Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Congress "United States Congress") passed legislation to divide the [Northwest Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Territory "Northwest Territory") into two areas and named the western section the [Indiana Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Territory "Indiana Territory"). In 1816, when Congress passed an Enabling Act to begin the process of establishing statehood for Indiana, a part of this territorial land became the geographic area for the new state.[[c]](#cite_note-22)[[20]](#cite_note-23)[[21]](#cite_note-IHBNI-24)

Formal use of the word *Indiana* dates from 1768, when a [Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia "Philadelphia")-based trading company gave its land claim in present-day [West Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Virginia "West Virginia") the name "Indiana" in honor of its previous owners, the [Iroquois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iroquois "Iroquois"). Later, ownership of the claim was transferred to the [Indiana Land Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Land_Company "Indiana Land Company"), the first recorded use of the word *Indiana*. But the [Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia "Virginia") colony argued that it was the rightful owner of the land because it fell within its geographic boundaries. The [U.S. Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Supreme_Court "United States Supreme Court") denied the land company's right to the claim in 1798.[[21]](#cite_note-IHBNI-24)

### Hoosier

A native or resident of Indiana is known as a [Hoosier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoosier "Hoosier").[[22]](#cite_note-25) The etymology of this word is disputed, but the leading theory, advanced by the Indiana Historical Bureau and the Indiana Historical Society, has its origin in Virginia, Kentucky, the Carolinas, and Tennessee (the [Upland South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upland_South "Upland South")) as a term for a backwoodsman, a rough countryman, or a country bumpkin.[[23]](#cite_note-26)[[24]](#cite_note-27)

## History

Main article: [History of Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Indiana "History of Indiana")

See also: [Outline of Indiana § History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outline_of_Indiana#History "Outline of Indiana")

### Indigenous inhabitants

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/Angel_mound_with_turret_HRoe_2008.jpg/250px-Angel_mound_with_turret_HRoe_2008.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Angel_mound_with_turret_HRoe_2008.jpg)

[Angel Mounds State Historic Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angel_Mounds_State_Historic_Site "Angel Mounds State Historic Site") was one of the northernmost [Mississippian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippian_culture "Mississippian culture") settlements, occupied from 1100 to 1450.

The first inhabitants in what is now Indiana were the [Paleo-Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleo-Indians "Paleo-Indians"), who arrived about 8000 BC after the melting of the glaciers at the end of the [Ice Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_Glacial_Period "Last Glacial Period"). Divided into small groups, the Paleo-Indians were [nomads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nomad "Nomad") who hunted large [game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game_(food) "Game (food)") such as [mastodons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mastodons "Mastodons"). They created stone tools made out of [chert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chert "Chert") by chipping, [knapping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knapping "Knapping") and [flaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithic_reduction "Lithic reduction").[[25]](#cite_note-HIS-28)

The [Archaic period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaic_period_in_the_Americas "Archaic period in the Americas") between 5000 and 4000 BC saw the development of new ground-stone tools the building of earthwork [mounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mound "Mound") and [middens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midden "Midden"), suggesting that settlements were becoming more permanent.[[25]](#cite_note-HIS-28) The [Woodland period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodland_period "Woodland period") began around 1500 BC marked by ceramics and pottery and the extended cultivation of plants eventually including crops such as [corn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize "Maize") and [squash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Squash_(plant) "Squash (plant)"), and the development of long-term trade, notably by the [Hopewell people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hopewell_tradition "Hopewell tradition").[[25]](#cite_note-HIS-28)

The [Mississippian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippian_culture "Mississippian culture") emerged, lasting from 1000 AD until the 15th century, shortly before the arrival of Europeans. During this stage, people created large urban settlements designed according to their [cosmology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmology "Cosmology"), with large mounds and plazas defining ceremonial and public spaces. The concentrated settlements depended on the agricultural surpluses. One such complex was the [Angel Mounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angel_Mounds "Angel Mounds"). They had large public areas such as plazas and platform mounds, where leaders lived or conducted rituals. Mississippian civilization collapsed in Indiana during the mid-15th century for reasons that remain unclear.[[25]](#cite_note-HIS-28)

The historic Native American tribes in the area at the time of European encounter spoke different languages of the [Algonquian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algonquian_languages "Algonquian languages") family. They included the [Shawnee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shawnee "Shawnee"), [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_people "Miami people"), and [Illini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illiniwek "Illiniwek"). Refugee tribes from eastern regions, including the [Delaware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_Nation "Delaware Nation") who settled in the White and Whitewater River Valleys, later joined them.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

### European exploration and sovereignty

See also: [New France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_France "New France"), [Louisiana (New France)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_(New_France) "Louisiana (New France)"), [Illinois Country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Country "Illinois Country"), and [Province of Quebec (1763–1791)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Quebec_(1763–1791) "Province of Quebec (1763–1791)")

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Natives_guiding_french_explorers_through_indiana.jpg/330px-Natives_guiding_french_explorers_through_indiana.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Natives_guiding_french_explorers_through_indiana.jpg)

Native Indians guide French explorers through Indiana, as depicted by [Maurice Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurice_Thompson "Maurice Thompson") in *Stories of Indiana*

In 1679, French explorer [René-Robert Cavelier, Sieur de La Salle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/René-Robert_Cavelier,_Sieur_de_La_Salle "René-Robert Cavelier, Sieur de La Salle") was the first European to cross into Indiana after reaching present-day [South Bend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Bend,_Indiana "South Bend, Indiana") at the [St. Joseph River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Joseph_River_(Lake_Michigan) "St. Joseph River (Lake Michigan)").[[26]](#cite_note-29) He was followed by French-Canadian [fur traders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fur_trade "Fur trade") exchanging blankets, jewelry, tools, whiskey and weapons with the Native Americans for skins.

By 1702, [Sieur Juchereau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Juchereau_de_St._Denis "Louis Juchereau de St. Denis") established the first trading post near [Vincennes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincennes,_Indiana "Vincennes, Indiana"). In 1715, [Sieur de Vincennes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Baptiste_Bissot,_Sieur_de_Vincennes "Jean Baptiste Bissot, Sieur de Vincennes") built [Fort Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Miami_(Indiana) "Fort Miami (Indiana)") at [Kekionga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kekionga "Kekionga"), now [Fort Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Wayne "Fort Wayne"). In 1717, another Canadian, [Picote de Beletre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Picote_de_Beletre "Picote de Beletre"), built [Fort Ouiatenon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Ouiatenon "Fort Ouiatenon") on the [Wabash River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wabash_River "Wabash River"), to try to control Native American trade routes from [Lake Erie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Erie "Lake Erie") to the [Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River "Mississippi River").

In 1732, Sieur de Vincennes built a second fur trading post at Vincennes. In a period of a few years, British colonists arrived from the East and contended with the Canadians for control of the lucrative fur trade. Fighting between the French and British colonists occurred throughout the 1750s.

The Native American tribes of Indiana sided with the French Canadians during the [French and Indian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_and_Indian_War "French and Indian War") (an episode of the [Seven Years' War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Years'_War "Seven Years' War") between the European [great powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_power "Great power")). With British victory in 1763, the French were forced to cede to the British crown all their lands in North America east of the Mississippi River and north and west of the [colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteen_Colonies "Thirteen Colonies").

Tribal resistance continued with [Pontiac's Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontiac's_Rebellion "Pontiac's Rebellion") and the capture of forts [Ouiatenon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Ouiatenon "Fort Ouiatenon") and [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forts_of_Fort_Wayne,_Indiana#Fort_Miami "Forts of Fort Wayne, Indiana"). The [Royal Proclamation of 1763](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Proclamation_of_1763 "Royal Proclamation of 1763") designated the land west of the [Appalachians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachians "Appalachians") as an [Indian Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Reserve_(1763) "Indian Reserve (1763)"), and excluded British colonists from the area, which the Crown called "Indian Territory".[[27]](#cite_note-30) The measure was one of the first significant areas of dispute between Britain and the [colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteen_Colonies "Thirteen Colonies") and would become a contributing factor leading to the [American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolution "American Revolution").[[28]](#cite_note-31)

In 1775, the [American Revolutionary War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War "American Revolutionary War") began as the colonists sought self-government and independence from the British. The majority of the fighting took place near the East Coast, but the Patriot military officer [George Rogers Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Rogers_Clark "George Rogers Clark") called for an army to help fight the British in the west.[[29]](#cite_note-32) Clark's army won significant battles and took over [Vincennes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincennes,_Indiana "Vincennes, Indiana") and [Fort Sackville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Sackville_(Vincennes) "Fort Sackville (Vincennes)") on February 25, 1779.[[30]](#cite_note-NO-33)

During the war, Clark managed to cut off British troops, who were attacking the eastern colonists from the west. His success is often credited for changing the course of the American Revolutionary War.[[31]](#cite_note-34) At the end of the war, through the [Treaty of Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_(1783) "Treaty of Paris (1783)"), the British crown ceded their claims to the land south of the Great Lakes to the newly formed United States, including Native American lands.

### The frontier

Main articles: [Northwest Ordinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Ordinance "Northwest Ordinance"), [Northwest Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Territory "Northwest Territory"), [Organic act § List of organic acts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organic_act#List_of_organic_acts "Organic act"), and [Indiana Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Territory "Indiana Territory")

[![A colorful map of Indiana with treaty names](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Indiana_Indian_treaties.svg/250px-Indiana_Indian_treaties.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Indiana_Indian_treaties.svg)

[![A crude map of Indiana with only a handful of southern counties delineated](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Indiana%2C_1817.jpg/250px-Indiana%2C_1817.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Indiana,_1817.jpg)

Left: A map showing extent of the treaty lands. Right: One of the first maps of Indiana (made 1816, published 1817) showing territories prior to the Treaty of St. Mary's which greatly expanded the region. Note the inaccurate placement of [Lake Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Michigan "Lake Michigan").

In 1787, the U.S. defined the [Northwest Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Territory "Northwest Territory") which included the area of present-day Indiana. In 1800, Congress separated [Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio "Ohio") from the Northwest Territory, designating the rest of the land as the [Indiana Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Territory "Indiana Territory").[[32]](#cite_note-GAC-35) President [Thomas Jefferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson "Thomas Jefferson") chose [William Henry Harrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Henry_Harrison "William Henry Harrison") as the governor of the territory, and [Vincennes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincennes,_Indiana "Vincennes, Indiana") was established as the capital.[[33]](#cite_note-36) After the [Michigan Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan_Territory "Michigan Territory") was separated and the [Illinois Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Territory "Illinois Territory") was formed, Indiana was reduced to its current size and geography.[[32]](#cite_note-GAC-35)

Starting with the [Battle of Fallen Timbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Fallen_Timbers "Battle of Fallen Timbers") in 1794 and the [Treaty of Greenville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Greenville "Treaty of Greenville") in 1795, [Native American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States "Native Americans in the United States") titles to Indiana lands were extinguished by usurpation, purchase, or war and treaty. About half the state was acquired in the [Treaty of St. Mary's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_St._Mary's "Treaty of St. Mary's") from the [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_people "Miami people") in 1818. Purchases were not complete until the [Treaty of Mississinewas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Mississinewas "Treaty of Mississinewas")[[34]](#cite_note-37) again with the Miami in 1826 acquired the last of the reserved Native American lands in the northeast.

By 1810, only two counties in the extreme southeast, [Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clark_County,_Indiana "Clark County, Indiana") and [Dearborn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dearborn_County,_Indiana "Dearborn County, Indiana"), had been organized by European settlers. Land titles issued out of Cincinnati were sparse. Settler migration was chiefly via flatboat on the Ohio River westerly, and by wagon trails up the Wabash/White River Valleys (west) and [Whitewater River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitewater_River_(Indiana) "Whitewater River (Indiana)") Valleys (east).

After working to maintain peaceful coexistence with the United States, the [Shawnee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shawnee "Shawnee") tribal chief [Tecumseh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tecumseh "Tecumseh") and his brother [Tenskwatawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenskwatawa "Tenskwatawa"), "the Shawnee Prophet", encouraged other indigenous tribes in the territory to reject European influences, stop drinking alcohol, and resist further encroachment. Tensions rose and the U.S. authorized Harrison to launch a preemptive expedition against [Tecumseh's Confederacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tecumseh's_Confederacy "Tecumseh's Confederacy"); the U.S. gained victory at the [Battle of Tippecanoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Tippecanoe "Battle of Tippecanoe") on November 7, 1811.[[35]](#cite_note-38)

In the [War of 1812](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_1812 "War of 1812"), the British, who had been assisted by Tecumseh in the [capture of Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Detroit "Siege of Detroit"), proposed creating of an [Indian barrier state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_barrier_state "Indian barrier state") to ensure the security of [Upper Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Canada "Upper Canada").[[36]](#cite_note-39) After Tecumseh was killed in 1813 at the [Battle of the Thames](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_the_Thames "Battle of the Thames"), resistance to United States control ended in the region. Most Native American tribes in the state were later [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_removals_in_Indiana "Indian removals in Indiana") to west of the [Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River "Mississippi River") in the 1820s and 1830s after being forced to accept the "purchase" of their lands.[[37]](#cite_note-40)

### Statehood and settlement

[Corydon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corydon,_Indiana "Corydon, Indiana"), a town in the far southern part of Indiana, was named the second capital of the Indiana Territory in May 1813 in order to decrease the threat of Native American raids following the Battle of Tippecanoe.[[32]](#cite_note-GAC-35) Two years later, a petition for statehood was approved by the territorial general assembly and sent to Congress. An [Enabling Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enabling_Act "Enabling Act") was passed to provide an election of delegates to write a [constitution for Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Indiana "Constitution of Indiana"). On June 10, 1816, delegates assembled at Corydon to write a state constitution, which was completed in 19 days. [Jonathan Jennings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Jennings "Jonathan Jennings") was elected the fledgling state's first governor in August 1816. President [James Madison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Madison "James Madison") approved Indiana's [admission into the union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admission_to_the_Union "Admission to the Union") as the [nineteenth state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_date_of_admission_to_the_Union "List of U.S. states by date of admission to the Union") on December 11, 1816.[[30]](#cite_note-NO-33) In 1825, the state capital was moved from Corydon to [Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis "Indianapolis").[[32]](#cite_note-GAC-35)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Indiana's Capitol Building in [Corydon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corydon,_Indiana "Corydon, Indiana") served as the state's seat of government from 1816 until 1825.[[38]](#cite_note-41) | 1950 postal issue of Harrison commemorating Indiana's 150th anniversary of statehood |

Many European [immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigrants "Immigrants") went west to settle in Indiana in the early 19th century. The largest immigrant group to settle in Indiana were [Germans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German-American "German-American") ([German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German-American "German-American") remains the largest ancestry reported in Indiana),[[39]](#cite_note-Germanic-42) as well as many immigrants from [Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ireland "Ireland") and [England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/England "England"). Americans who were primarily [ethnically English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_American "English American") migrated from the Northern Tier of [New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_(state) "New York (state)") and [New England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England "New England"), as well as from the mid-Atlantic state of [Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania "Pennsylvania").[[40]](#cite_note-The_H-43)[[41]](#cite_note-44) The arrival of steamboats on the Ohio River in 1811, and the [National Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Road "National Road") at Richmond in 1829, greatly facilitated settlement of northern and western Indiana.

Following statehood, the new government worked to transform Indiana from a [frontier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_frontier "American frontier") into a developed, well-populated, and thriving state, beginning significant demographic and economic changes. In 1836, the state's founders initiated a program, the [Indiana Mammoth Internal Improvement Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Mammoth_Internal_Improvement_Act "Indiana Mammoth Internal Improvement Act"), that led to the construction of roads, [canals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canal "Canal"), railroads and state-funded public schools. The plans bankrupted the state and were a financial disaster, but increased land and produce value more than fourfold.[[42]](#cite_note-45) In response to the crisis and in order to avert another, in 1851, a second [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Indiana "Constitution of Indiana") was adopted, which prohibited public debt. At the same time, however, it included a mandate for a "uniform system of common schools, equally open to all and free of tuition."[[43]](#cite_note-46)

In a reaction against an influx of [free people of color](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_people_of_color "Free people of color") and emancipated slaves who had been expelled from slave states, Article 13 of the new constitution sought to bar their further immigration into Indiana and proposed their resettlement in [Liberia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Liberia "Republic of Liberia").[[44]](#cite_note-Madison49-47) Citing the newly passed [Thirteenth Amendment to the U.S. Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteenth_Amendment_to_the_U.S._Constitution "Thirteenth Amendment to the U.S. Constitution"), the Indiana Supreme Court struck down the article in 1866, and it was removed by amendment in 1881.[[45]](#cite_note-48)[[46]](#cite_note-Shephard40-41-49) Nevertheless, numerous communities and counties implemented practices to exclude [African Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Americans "African Americans"). These jurisdictions, known as "[sundown towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sundown_town "Sundown town")", were prevalent during the 1890s.[[47]](#cite_note-50)[[48]](#cite_note-51)

### Civil War and late 19th-century industry

Main article: [Indiana in the American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_in_the_American_Civil_War "Indiana in the American Civil War")

Indiana was the first western state to mobilize for the United States in the [American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War "American Civil War"), and soldiers from Indiana participated in all the war's major engagements. In 1861, Indiana was assigned a [quota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War#Mobilization "American Civil War") of 7,500 soldiers to join the [Union Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Army "Union Army").[[49]](#cite_note-52) So many volunteered in the first call that thousands had to be turned away. Before the war ended, Indiana had contributed 208,367 organized in 126 infantry regiments, 26 batteries of artillery and 13 regiments of cavalry to the [Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_(American_Civil_War) "Union (American Civil War)").[[50]](#cite_note-53) Casualties were over 35% of the enlisted: 24,416 men lost their lives and over 50,000 more were wounded.[[51]](#cite_note-54) The only Civil War conflicts fought in Indiana were the [Newburgh Raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newburgh_Raid "Newburgh Raid"), a bloodless capture of the city; and the [Battle of Corydon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Corydon "Battle of Corydon"), which occurred during [Morgan's Raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgan's_Raid "Morgan's Raid") leaving 15 dead, 40 wounded, and 355 captured.[[52]](#cite_note-55)

After the war, Indiana remained a largely agricultural state. Post-war industries included mining, including limestone extraction; meatpacking; food processing, such as milling grain, distilling it into alcohol; and the building of [wagons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagon "Wagon"), [buggies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buggy_(carriage) "Buggy (carriage)"), farm machinery, and hardware.[[53]](#cite_note-INdustry-56) However, the [discovery of natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_gas_boom "Indiana gas boom") in the 1880s in northern Indiana led to an economic boom: the abundant and cheap fuel attracted [heavy industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_industry "Heavy industry"); the availability of jobs, in turn, attracted new settlers from other parts of the country as well as from Europe.[[54]](#cite_note-Gray_1995,_p._202-57) This led to the rapid expansion of cities such as [South Bend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Bend "South Bend"), Indianapolis, and [Fort Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Wayne "Fort Wayne").[[53]](#cite_note-INdustry-56)

### Early 20th century

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Midnight_at_the_glassworks2b.jpg/250px-Midnight_at_the_glassworks2b.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Midnight_at_the_glassworks2b.jpg)

Child labor in Indiana glassworks, 1908, by [Lewis Hine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hine "Lewis Hine")

The early decades of the 20th century saw Indiana develop into a leading [manufacturing state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rust_Belt "Rust Belt") with heavy industry concentrating in the north.[[40]](#cite_note-The_H-43) In 1906 the [United States Steel Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Steel_Corporation "United States Steel Corporation") created a new industrial city on Lake Michigan named [Gary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary,_Indiana "Gary, Indiana"), after [Elbert Henry Gary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elbert_Henry_Gary "Elbert Henry Gary"), its founding chairman. With industrialization, workers developed labor unions (their strike activities induced governor [James P. Goodrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_P._Goodrich "James P. Goodrich") to declare martial law in Gary in 1919)[[55]](#cite_note-58) and a [Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Party_of_America "Socialist Party of America").[[56]](#cite_note-59) Railroader [Eugene Debs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_V._Debs "Eugene V. Debs") of [Terre Haute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terre_Haute,_Indiana "Terre Haute, Indiana"), the Socialist candidate received 901,551 votes (6.0% of the national vote) in the 1912 presidential election.[[57]](#cite_note-Election1912-60) Suffrage movements also arose to enfranchise women.[[54]](#cite_note-Gray_1995,_p._202-57)

In its earlier years, Indiana was a leader in the automobile boom. Beginning its production in [Kokomo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokomo,_Indiana "Kokomo, Indiana") in 1896, [Haynes-Apperson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haynes-Apperson "Haynes-Apperson") was the nation's first commercially successful auto company.[[58]](#cite_note-61) The importance of vehicle and parts manufacture to the state was symbolized by the construction in 1909 of the [Indianapolis Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Motor_Speedway "Indianapolis Motor Speedway").[[59]](#cite_note-history-62)

In the 1920s, state politics was heavily influenced by the rise of the [Indiana Klan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Klan "Indiana Klan"). First organized in 1915 as a branch of the [Ku Klux Klan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ku_Klux_Klan "Ku Klux Klan"), it appealed to white Protestants alarmed by social and economic trends, including changes induced by immigration from southern and central Europe.[[60]](#cite_note-63) In the name of defending "hundred-per-cent Americanism", the Klan sought to exclude from public life "[Bolsheviks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism "Communism"), [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church "Catholic Church"), [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews "Jews"), [Negroes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negro "Negro"), [bootleggers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rum-running "Rum-running"), [pacifists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacifism "Pacifism"), [evolutionists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evolutionism "Evolutionism"), foreigners, and all persons it considered immoral".[[61]](#cite_note-64)

By 1925 the Klan had 250,000 members, an estimated 30% of native-born white men.[[62]](#cite_note-65)[[63]](#cite_note-66) By 1925 over half the elected members of the [Indiana General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_General_Assembly "Indiana General Assembly"), the [governor of Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Indiana "Governor of Indiana"), and many other high-ranking officials in local and state government were members of the Klan. Politicians had also learned they needed Klan endorsement to win office.[[64]](#cite_note-Moore-1991-67) That year, "Grand Dragon" [D.C. Stephenson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D.C._Stephenson "D.C. Stephenson"), who had begun to brag "I am the law in Indiana",[[65]](#cite_note-Lutholtz-1991-68) was charged and convicted for the rape and murder of [Madge Oberholtzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madge_Oberholtzer "Madge Oberholtzer"), a young school teacher. Denied pardon, in 1927 Stephenson gave the *[Indianapolis Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Times "Indianapolis Times")* lists of people the Klan had paid. Partly as a result of compounded scandal, membership collapsed.[[66]](#cite_note-Lutholtz2-69)

Throughout the 1930s, [New Deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Deal "New Deal") [Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_(United_States) "Democratic Party (United States)") topped the polls[[67]](#cite_note-Madison-2014-70) and "the Klan was political poison".[[68]](#cite_note-71) During those years, Indiana, like the rest of the nation, was affected by the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression "Great Depression"): businesses were shuttered and farm income collapsed. The numbers seeking employment, shelter and relief were augmented by out-of-state [Dust Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dust_Bowl "Dust Bowl") migrants. Swept into office in the nationwide landslide for [Franklin Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt "Franklin D. Roosevelt") in 1932, Democratic governor [Paul V. McNutt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_V._McNutt "Paul V. McNutt"), called on Hoosiers to "prove that government may be a great instrument of human progress",[[67]](#cite_note-Madison-2014-70) and struggled to build a state-funded welfare system to help overwhelmed private charities. He reorganized state government, greatly enlarging his executive powers, introduced state income tax and ended [Prohibition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prohibition_in_the_United_States "Prohibition in the United States"). On several occasions, he declared [martial law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_law "Martial law") to put an end to worker strikes.[[69]](#cite_note-72)

[World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II "World War II") helped lift Indiana's economy, as the war required steel, food and other goods the state produced.[[70]](#cite_note-TO-73) Roughly 10% of Indiana's population joined the armed forces, while hundreds of industries earned war production contracts and began making war material.[[71]](#cite_note-74) Indiana manufactured 4.5% of total U.S. military armaments during World War II, ranking eighth among the 48 states.[[72]](#cite_note-75) The expansion of industry to meet war demands helped end the Great Depression.[[70]](#cite_note-TO-73)[[73]](#cite_note-76)

### Post World War II

With economic recovery, the Republican supremacy in the state was restored.[[74]](#cite_note-77) Manufacturers became the primary employers, a trend that continued into the 1960s. [Urbanization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbanization "Urbanization") during the 1950s and 1960s led to substantial growth in the state's cities. The auto, steel and pharmaceutical industries topped Indiana's major businesses. Indiana's population continued to grow after the war, exceeding five million by the 1970 census.[[75]](#cite_note-78)

In the early 1960s, Democrats briefly returned to state office, and under the administration of [Matthew E. Welsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_E._Welsh "Matthew E. Welsh") the state adopted its first sales tax of 2%.[[76]](#cite_note-79) Indiana schools were [desegregated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desegregated "Desegregated") in 1949. In 1950, the [U.S. Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Census_Bureau "United States Census Bureau") reported Indiana's population as 95.5% white and 4.4% black.[[77]](#cite_note-USCB-80) Governor Welsh also worked with the General Assembly to pass the Indiana [Civil Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Rights "Civil Rights") Bill, granting equal protection to minorities in seeking employment.[[78]](#cite_note-81)

The [1973 oil crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_oil_crisis "1973 oil crisis") created a recession that hurt the automotive industry in Indiana. Companies began a long series of downsizing that contributed to high unemployment rates in long-established manufacturing centers such as [Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anderson,_Indiana "Anderson, Indiana"), [Gary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary,_Indiana "Gary, Indiana"), [Muncie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muncie,_Indiana "Muncie, Indiana"), and [Kokomo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokomo,_Indiana "Kokomo, Indiana"). The trend continued until the 1980s when the national and state economy began to diversify from heavy industry toward services and high-tech manufacturing, and to recover.[[79]](#cite_note-82) Meanwhile, the [farm crisis of the 1980s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980s_farm_crisis "1980s farm crisis") accelerated [rural flight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rural_flight "Rural flight") and farm consolidation,[[80]](#cite_note-83) a trend that has resumed in the 21st century.[[81]](#cite_note-84)[[82]](#cite_note-85)

### 21st century

On March 6, 2020, following the first confirmed case of [COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19 "COVID-19") in the state, Governor [Eric Holcomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Holcomb "Eric Holcomb") and state health officials declared a public health emergency. After a period in which there were a number of [pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic")-related restrictions and advisories, and sharp job cuts,[[83]](#cite_note-86) Holcomb signed a bill to end the emergency on March 3, 2022.[[84]](#cite_note-87)

At that point, employment had already recovered,[[85]](#cite_note-88) but in comparative terms state appeared, post-COVID, to face greater challenges. In 2025 Indiana's economy (the state's business environment, labor market and overall economic growth) ranked 40th in the first [U.S. News & World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._News_&_World_Report "U.S. News & World Report") Report's annual report on how well all 50 states in the United States serve citizens,[[86]](#cite_note-USWR_Best_State_Rankings_2025-89) down from 25th in 2018.[[87]](#cite_note-90)

Indiana's highest ranking on the 2025 "scorecard" was 16th in the opportunity category. It was 30th in economic opportunity, and 39th in equality, but benefitted from being 18th in affordability (by comparison New Hampshire, which was 1st in economic opportunity and 3rd in equality, was 42nd in affordability).[[86]](#cite_note-USWR_Best_State_Rankings_2025-89) Its lowest score was for pollution and natural environment, with Indiana ranking worst among the 50 states.[[88]](#cite_note-91)

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Indiana "Geography of Indiana")

With a total area (land and water) of 36,418 square miles (94,320 km2), Indiana ranks as the 38th largest state in size.[[89]](#cite_note-NatAtlasProfile-92) The state has a maximum dimension north to south of 250 miles (400 km) and a maximum east to west dimension of 145 miles (233 km).[[90]](#cite_note-93) The state's geographic center (39° 53.7'N, 86° 16.0W) is in [Marion County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana "Marion County, Indiana").[[91]](#cite_note-Netstate-94)

Located in the [Midwestern United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States "Midwestern United States"), Indiana is one of eight states that make up the [Great Lakes Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Lakes_region_(North_America) "Great Lakes region (North America)").[[92]](#cite_note-95) Indiana is bordered on the north by [Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan "Michigan"), on the east by [Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio "Ohio"), and on the west by [Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois "Illinois"), partially separated by the [Wabash River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wabash_River "Wabash River").[[93]](#cite_note-FW-96) [Lake Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Michigan "Lake Michigan") borders Indiana on the northwest and the [Ohio River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_River "Ohio River") separates Indiana from [Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kentucky "Kentucky") on the south.[[91]](#cite_note-Netstate-94)[[94]](#cite_note-97)

### Geology and terrain

See also: [Paleontology in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleontology_in_Indiana "Paleontology in Indiana"), [List of ecoregions in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ecoregions_in_Indiana "List of ecoregions in Indiana"), and [List of wildflowers in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_wildflowers_in_Indiana "List of wildflowers in Indiana")

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/HickoryRidgeTower-NW.jpg/250px-HickoryRidgeTower-NW.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:HickoryRidgeTower-NW.jpg)

Rolling hills in the [Charles C. Deam Wilderness Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_C._Deam_Wilderness_Area "Charles C. Deam Wilderness Area") of [Hoosier National Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoosier_National_Forest "Hoosier National Forest"), in the [Indiana Uplands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Uplands "Indiana Uplands")

The average altitude of Indiana is about 760 feet (230 m) above sea level.[[95]](#cite_note-98) The highest point in the state is [Hoosier Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoosier_Hill "Hoosier Hill") in [Wayne County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayne_County,_Indiana "Wayne County, Indiana") at 1,257 feet (383 m) above sea level.[[89]](#cite_note-NatAtlasProfile-92)[[96]](#cite_note-99) The lowest point at 320 feet (98 m) above sea level is in [Posey County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Posey_County,_Indiana "Posey County, Indiana"), where the [Wabash River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wabash_River "Wabash River") meets the [Ohio River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_River "Ohio River").[[89]](#cite_note-NatAtlasProfile-92)[[91]](#cite_note-Netstate-94) The resulting [elevation span](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_elevation "List of U.S. states and territories by elevation"), 937 feet (286 m), is the narrowest of any non-coastal U.S. state. Only 2,850 square miles (7,400 km2) have an altitude greater than 1,000 feet (300 m) and this area is enclosed within 14 counties. About 4,700 square miles (12,000 km2) have an elevation of less than 500 feet (150 m), mostly concentrated along the Ohio and lower Wabash Valleys, from [Tell City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tell_City,_Indiana "Tell City, Indiana") and [Terre Haute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terre_Haute,_Indiana "Terre Haute, Indiana") to [Evansville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evansville,_Indiana "Evansville, Indiana") and [Mount Vernon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Vernon,_Indiana "Mount Vernon, Indiana").[[97]](#cite_note-100)

The state includes two natural regions of the United States: the Central Lowlands and the [Interior Low Plateaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interior_Low_Plateaus "Interior Low Plateaus").[[98]](#cite_note-101) The [till plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Till_plain "Till plain") make up the northern and central regions of Indiana. Much of its appearance is a result of elements left behind by glaciers. Central Indiana is mainly flat with some low rolling hills (except where rivers cut deep valleys through the plain, like at the Wabash River and Sugar Creek) and soil composed of glacial sands, gravel and clay, which results in exceptional farmland.[[93]](#cite_note-FW-96) Northern Indiana is similar, except for the presence of higher and hillier [terminal moraines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminal_moraine "Terminal moraine") and hundreds of [kettle lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kettle_lake "Kettle lake"). In [northwest Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Indiana "Northwest Indiana") there are various sand ridges and dunes, some reaching nearly 200 feet in height; most of them are at [Indiana Dunes National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Dunes_National_Park "Indiana Dunes National Park"). These are along the Lake Michigan shoreline and also inland to the [Kankakee Outwash Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kankakee_Outwash_Plain "Kankakee Outwash Plain").

Southern Indiana is characterized by valleys and rugged, hilly terrain, contrasting with much of the state. Here, bedrock is exposed at the surface. Because of the prevalent [Indiana limestone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_limestone "Indiana limestone"), the area has many caves, caverns, and quarries. Harrison Spring, the largest spring in the state, is located in Harrison County.

### Hydrology

See also: [List of Indiana rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indiana_rivers "List of Indiana rivers"), [List of dams and reservoirs in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_dams_and_reservoirs_in_Indiana "List of dams and reservoirs in Indiana"), [List of lakes in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lakes_in_Indiana "List of lakes in Indiana"), and [Watersheds of Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watersheds_of_Indiana "Watersheds of Indiana")

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Floods_Recede_around_the_Wabash-Ohio_Confluence.jpg/250px-Floods_Recede_around_the_Wabash-Ohio_Confluence.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Floods_Recede_around_the_Wabash-Ohio_Confluence.jpg)

The [Wabash River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wabash_River "Wabash River") converges with the [Ohio River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_River "Ohio River") at [Posey County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Posey_County,_Indiana "Posey County, Indiana"). The southernmost point in Indiana is located at Uniontown Bend 4.6 miles upstream of the confluence, south of [Hovey Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hovey_Lake "Hovey Lake") (near the center of the photograph)

Major river systems in Indiana include the Whitewater, White, Blue, Wabash, St. Joseph, and Maumee rivers.[[99]](#cite_note-102) According to the Indiana Department of Natural Resources, as of 2007, there were 65 rivers, streams, and creeks of environmental interest or scenic beauty, which included only a portion of an estimated 24,000 total river miles within the state.[[100]](#cite_note-103)

The [Wabash River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wabash_River "Wabash River"), which is the longest free-flowing river east of the [Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River "Mississippi River"), is the official river of Indiana.[[101]](#cite_note-104)[[102]](#cite_note-105) At 475 miles (764 kilometers) in length, the river bisects the state from northeast to southwest, forming part of the state's border with Illinois, before converging with the [Ohio River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_River "Ohio River"). The river has been the subject of several songs, such as "[On the Banks of the Wabash, Far Away](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On_the_Banks_of_the_Wabash,_Far_Away "On the Banks of the Wabash, Far Away")", "[Wabash Cannonball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wabash_Cannonball "Wabash Cannonball")", and "[Back Home Again in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Back_Home_Again_in_Indiana "Back Home Again in Indiana")".[[103]](#cite_note-106)[[104]](#cite_note-107)

There are about 900 lakes listed by the Indiana Department of Natural Resources.[[105]](#cite_note-108) To the northwest, Indiana borders [Lake Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Michigan "Lake Michigan"), one of five lakes comprising the [Great Lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Lakes "Great Lakes"), the largest group of freshwater lakes in the world. [Tippecanoe Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tippecanoe_Lake "Tippecanoe Lake"), the deepest lake in the state, reaches depths at nearly 120 feet (37 m), while [Lake Wawasee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Wawasee "Lake Wawasee") is the largest natural lake in Indiana.[[106]](#cite_note-109) At 10,750 acres (summer pool level), [Monroe Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monroe_Lake "Monroe Lake") is the largest lake in Indiana.[[107]](#cite_note-110)

### Climate

Further information: [Climate change in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Indiana "Climate change in Indiana")

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/K%C3%B6ppen_Climate_Types_Indiana.png/250px-K%C3%B6ppen_Climate_Types_Indiana.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Köppen_Climate_Types_Indiana.png)

[Köppen climate types](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Köppen_climate_classification "Köppen climate classification") of Indiana, using 1991–2020 [climate normals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climatological_normal "Climatological normal")

In the past, almost all of Indiana had a [humid continental climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_continental_climate "Humid continental climate") ([Dfa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Köppen_climate_classification "Köppen climate classification")), with cold winters and hot, wet summers;[[108]](#cite_note-111) only the extreme southern portion of the state lay within the [humid subtropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate "Humid subtropical climate") ([Cfa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Köppen_climate_classification "Köppen climate classification")), which receives more precipitation than other parts of Indiana.[[93]](#cite_note-FW-96) But as of the 2016 update, about half the state is now classified as humid subtropical. Temperatures generally diverge from the north and south sections of the state. In midwinter, average high/low temperatures range from around 30 °F/15 °F (−1 °C/−10 °C) in the far north to 41 °F/24 °F (5 °C/−4 °C) in the far south.[[109]](#cite_note-weather.gov-112)

In midsummer there is generally a little less variation across the state, as average high/low temperatures range from around 84 °F/64 °F (29 °C/18 °C) in the far north to 90 °F/69 °F (32 °C/21 °C) in the far south.[[109]](#cite_note-weather.gov-112) Indiana's record high temperature was 116 °F (47 °C) set on July 14, 1936, at [Collegeville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collegeville,_Indiana "Collegeville, Indiana"). The record low was −36 °F (−38 °C) on [January 19, 1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_North_American_cold_wave "1994 North American cold wave") at [New Whiteland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Whiteland,_Indiana "New Whiteland, Indiana").[[110]](#cite_note-113) The [growing season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Growing_season "Growing season") typically spans from 155 days in the north to 185 days in the south.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

While droughts occasionally occur in the state, rainfall totals are distributed relatively equally throughout the year. Precipitation totals range from 35 inches (89 cm) near Lake Michigan in northwest Indiana to 45 inches (110 cm) along the Ohio River in the south, while the state's average is 40 inches (100 cm). Annual snowfall in Indiana varies widely across the state, ranging from 80 inches (200 cm) in the northwest along Lake Michigan to 14 inches (36 cm) in the far south. [Lake effect snow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_effect_snow "Lake effect snow") accounts for roughly half the snowfall in northwest and north central Indiana due to the effects of the moisture and relative warmth of Lake Michigan upwind. The average wind speed ranges from 6.5 miles per hour (10.5 km/h) to 12.7 miles per hour (20.4 km/h).[[111]](#cite_note-114)

In a 2012 report, Indiana was ranked eighth in a list of the top 20 tornado-prone states based on [National Weather Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Weather_Service "National Weather Service") data from 1950 through 2011.[[112]](#cite_note-115) A 2011 report ranked [South Bend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Bend,_Indiana "South Bend, Indiana") 15th among the top 20 tornado-prone U.S. cities,[[113]](#cite_note-116) while another report from 2011 ranked [Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis "Indianapolis") eighth.[[114]](#cite_note-117)[[d]](#cite_note-118)[[e]](#cite_note-119)Despite its vulnerability, Indiana is not part of [Tornado Alley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tornado_Alley "Tornado Alley").[[115]](#cite_note-120)

### Time zones

Main article: [Time in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indiana "Time in Indiana")

Indiana is one of 13 U.S. states that are divided into more than one time zone. Indiana's [time zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_zone "Time zone") have fluctuated over the past century. At present, most of the state observes [Eastern Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Time_Zone "Eastern Time Zone"); six counties near Chicago and six near Evansville observe [Central Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Time_Zone_(North_America) "Central Time Zone (North America)").[[118]](#cite_note-123) Debate continues on the matter.[[119]](#cite_note-124)

Before 2006, most of Indiana did not observe [daylight saving time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daylight_saving_time "Daylight saving time") (DST). Some counties within this area, particularly [Floyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Floyd_County,_Indiana "Floyd County, Indiana"), [Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clark_County,_Indiana "Clark County, Indiana"), and [Harrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrison_County,_Indiana "Harrison County, Indiana") counties near [Louisville, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisville,_Kentucky "Louisville, Kentucky"), and [Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_County,_Indiana "Ohio County, Indiana") and [Dearborn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dearborn_County,_Indiana "Dearborn County, Indiana") counties near [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati "Cincinnati"), Ohio, unofficially observed DST by local custom. Since April 2006 the entire state observes DST.[[120]](#cite_note-125)

### Indiana counties and statistical areas

See also: [List of counties in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Indiana "List of counties in Indiana") and [Indiana statistical areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_statistical_areas "Indiana statistical areas")

Indiana is divided into 92 [counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Indiana "List of counties in Indiana"). As of 2010[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indiana&action=edit), the state includes 16 [metropolitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_statistical_area "Metropolitan statistical area") and 25 [micropolitan statistical areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micropolitan_statistical_area "Micropolitan statistical area"), 117 incorporated cities, 450 towns, and several other smaller divisions and statistical areas.[[121]](#cite_note-GeoCensus-126)[[f]](#cite_note-127) [Marion County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana "Marion County, Indiana") and Indianapolis have a [consolidated city-county government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unigov "Unigov") known as Unigov in Indiana state law.[[121]](#cite_note-GeoCensus-126)

#### Major cities

See also: [List of municipalities in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Indiana "List of municipalities in Indiana")

Indianapolis is the capital of Indiana and its largest city.[[121]](#cite_note-GeoCensus-126)[[g]](#cite_note-128) Indiana's four largest metropolitan areas are [Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis "Indianapolis"), [Fort Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Wayne "Fort Wayne"), [Evansville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evansville "Evansville"), and [South Bend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Bend "South Bend").[[122]](#cite_note-STATSIN-129) The table below lists the state's twenty largest municipalities based on the 2020 United States census.[[123]](#cite_note-census.indiana.edu-130)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Indiana_population_pyramid.webp/250px-Indiana_population_pyramid.webp?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Indiana_population_pyramid.webp)

Indiana [population pyramid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_pyramid "Population pyramid")

Indiana recorded a population of 6,785,528 in the [2020 United States census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census "2020 United States census"), a 4.65% increase since the [2010 United States census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_census "2010 United States census").[[125]](#cite_note-2020_U.S._Census-132)

The state's population density was 70/km2 or 181/mi2, the 16th-highest in the United States.[[121]](#cite_note-GeoCensus-126) As of the 2010 U.S. census, Indiana's population center was northwest of Sheridan, in [Hamilton County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamilton_County,_Indiana "Hamilton County, Indiana") (+40.149246, −086.259514).[[121]](#cite_note-GeoCensus-126)[[126]](#cite_note-133)[[h]](#cite_note-134)

In 2005, 77.7% of Indiana residents lived in metropolitan counties, 16.5% lived in micropolitan counties and 5.9% lived in non-core counties.[[127]](#cite_note-135)

According to [HUD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Housing_and_Urban_Development "United States Department of Housing and Urban Development")'s 2022 [Annual Homeless Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annual_Homeless_Assessment_Report_to_Congress "Annual Homeless Assessment Report to Congress"), there were an estimated 5,449 [homeless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness "Homelessness") people in Indiana.[[128]](#cite_note-136)[[129]](#cite_note-137)

In 2018, the top countries of origin for Indiana's immigrants were [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico "Mexico"), [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India "India"), [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China "China"), [Myanmar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myanmar "Myanmar"), and the [Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines "Philippines").[[130]](#cite_note-138) The city of Fort Wayne in particular is home to one of the largest communities of Burmese immigrants in the country.[[131]](#cite_note-139)

| [Two or more races](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiracial_American "Multiracial American") | – | 1.2% | 2.0% | 6.4% |

[German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German-American "German-American") is the largest ancestry reported in Indiana, with 18.8% of the population reporting that ancestry in the census. Persons listing themselves as [American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_ancestry "American ancestry") (7.2%) and those of [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English-American "English-American") ancestry (11.1%) are also numerous, as are [Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_American "Irish American") (9.8%) and [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poland "Poland") (2.6%).[[39]](#cite_note-Germanic-42) Most of those citing American ancestry are actually of European descent, including many of English descent, but have family that has been in North America for so long, in many cases since the early [colonial era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_America "British America"), that they identify simply as American.[[140]](#cite_note-149)[[141]](#cite_note-150)[[142]](#cite_note-151)[[143]](#cite_note-152) In the 1980 census 1,776,144 people claimed German ancestry, 1,356,135 claimed English ancestry and 1,017,944 claimed Irish ancestry out of a total population of 4,241,975 making the state 42% German, 32% English and 24% Irish.[[144]](#cite_note-153)

The state is home to a growing Hispanic population, making up 7.8% of the total population. The largest Hispanic ancestry in the state is Mexican (5.3%), making up a large majority of the Hispanic population.[[145]](#cite_note-154)

In the [2020 Census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_Census "2020 United States Census"), 648,513 Indiana residents were identified as African American (of the total 6,785,528).[[146]](#cite_note-155) In five of the state's 92 counties, African Americans make up more than 10% of the population: [Marion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana "Marion County, Indiana") (27.5%), [Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_County,_Indiana "Lake County, Indiana") (24.9%), [St. Joseph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Joseph_County,_Indiana "St. Joseph County, Indiana") (13.2%), [Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allen_County,_Indiana "Allen County, Indiana") (11.5%), and [LaPorte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LaPorte_County,_Indiana "LaPorte County, Indiana") (10.8%). African Americans in the seven counties of Marion (268,694), Lake (124,073), Allen (44,481), St. Joseph (36,123), [Vanderburgh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanderburgh_County,_Indiana "Vanderburgh County, Indiana") (17,668), [Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamilton_County,_Indiana "Hamilton County, Indiana") (14,993), and [Hendricks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hendricks_County,_Indiana "Hendricks County, Indiana") (13,521) make up more than 80% of all African Americans in the state.[[147]](#cite_note-156)

### Population growth

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Indiana_population_density_2020.png/250px-Indiana_population_density_2020.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Indiana_population_density_2020.png)

Population density in Indiana as of the 2020 U.S. census

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Indiana_counties_by_race.svg/250px-Indiana_counties_by_race.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Indiana_counties_by_race.svg)

Map of counties in Indiana by racial plurality, per the 2020 U.S. census

|  |
| --- |
| **Non-Hispanic White** 50–60%  60–70%  70–80%  80–90%  90%+ |

Population growth since 1990 has been concentrated in the counties surrounding Indianapolis, with four of the five fastest-growing counties in that area: [Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamilton_County,_Indiana "Hamilton County, Indiana"), [Hendricks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hendricks_County,_Indiana "Hendricks County, Indiana"), [Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_County,_Indiana "Johnson County, Indiana"), and [Hancock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hancock_County,_Indiana "Hancock County, Indiana"). The other county is [Dearborn County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dearborn_County,_Indiana "Dearborn County, Indiana"), which is near [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati "Cincinnati"), [Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio "Ohio"). Hamilton County has also grown faster than any county in the states bordering Indiana ([Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois "Illinois"), [Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan "Michigan"), [Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio "Ohio") and [Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kentucky "Kentucky")), and is the 20th-fastest growing county in the country.[[148]](#cite_note-157)

With a population of 887,642, [Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis "Indianapolis") is the largest city in Indiana and the 15th-largest in the United States, according to the 2020 census. Three other cities in Indiana have a population greater than 100,000: [Fort Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Wayne,_Indiana "Fort Wayne, Indiana") (253,617), [Evansville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evansville,_Indiana "Evansville, Indiana") (117,429) and [South Bend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Bend,_Indiana "South Bend, Indiana") (101,168).[[149]](#cite_note-158) Since 2000, [Fishers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishers,_Indiana "Fishers, Indiana") has seen the largest population rise amongst the state's twenty largest cities with an increase of 100%.[[150]](#cite_note-Nevers-159) Other cities that have seen extensive growth since 2000 are [Greenwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenwood,_Indiana "Greenwood, Indiana") (81%), [Noblesville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noblesville,_Indiana "Noblesville, Indiana") (39.4%), [Carmel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmel,_Indiana "Carmel, Indiana") (21.4%), [Columbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbus,_Indiana "Columbus, Indiana")[[151]](#cite_note-wwwcensusgov-160) (12.8%) and [Lawrence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence,_Indiana "Lawrence, Indiana") (9.3%).

[Gary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary,_Indiana "Gary, Indiana") and [Hammond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hammond,_Indiana "Hammond, Indiana") have had the largest population declines regarding the 20 largest cities since 2000, with a decrease of 21.0% and 6.8% respectively.[[150]](#cite_note-Nevers-159) [Evansville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evansville,_Indiana "Evansville, Indiana") (−4.2%), [Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anderson,_Indiana "Anderson, Indiana") (−4.0%) and [Muncie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muncie,_Indiana "Muncie, Indiana") (−3.9%) have also had declines.[[152]](#cite_note-161)

Indianapolis has the largest population of the state's [metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_area "Metropolitan area") and the [33rd-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_of_United_States_Metropolitan_Statistical_Areas "Table of United States Metropolitan Statistical Areas") in the country.[[153]](#cite_note-162) The [Indianapolis metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_metropolitan_area "Indianapolis metropolitan area") encompasses [Marion County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana "Marion County, Indiana") and nine surrounding counties in central Indiana.

*Note: Births in table don't add up, because Hispanics are counted both by their ethnicity and by their race, giving a higher overall number.*

Based on population estimates for 2011, 6.6% of the state's population is under the age of five, 24.5% is under the age of 18, and 13.2% is 65 years of age or older. From the 2010 U.S. census demographic data for Indiana, the median age is 37.[[165]](#cite_note-AmFacts-174)

### Median income

See also: [Indiana locations by per capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_locations_by_per_capita_income "Indiana locations by per capita income")

As of the [2010 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_Census "2010 United States Census"), Indiana's median household income was $44,616, ranking it 36th among the United States and the District of Columbia.[[166]](#cite_note-175) In 2005, the median household income for Indiana residents was $43,993. Nearly 498,700 Indiana households had incomes between $50,000 and $75,000, accounting for 20% of all households.[[167]](#cite_note-Justis_2006-176)

Hamilton County's median household income is nearly $35,000 higher than the Indiana average. At $78,932, it ranks seventh in the country among counties with fewer than 250,000 people. The next highest median incomes in Indiana are also found in the Indianapolis suburbs; Hendricks County has a median of $57,538, followed by Johnson County at $56,251.[[167]](#cite_note-Justis_2006-176)

### Religion

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Jrb_20071024_Mennonite_Amish_buggy_Shipshewana_Indiana.JPG/250px-Jrb_20071024_Mennonite_Amish_buggy_Shipshewana_Indiana.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jrb_20071024_Mennonite_Amish_buggy_Shipshewana_Indiana.JPG)

Indiana is home to the [third largest population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amish_in_Indiana "Amish in Indiana") of [Amish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amish "Amish") in the U.S.[[168]](#cite_note-177)

Although the largest single religious denomination in the state is Catholic (747,706 members), most Hoosiers are members of various Protestant denominations. The largest Protestant denomination by number of adherents in 2010 was the [United Methodist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Methodist_Church "United Methodist Church"), with 355,043.[[169]](#cite_note-www.thearda.com-178) A study by the Graduate Center at the [City University of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_University_of_New_York "City University of New York") found 20% are Catholic, 14% belong to [Baptist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptist "Baptist") churches, 10% are other Christians, 9% are [Methodist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methodist "Methodist"), and 6% are [Lutheran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheran "Lutheran"). About 1% are [Old Order Amish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amish "Amish"), the [highest percentage of all US states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_Amish_population#Statistics_of_states "List of U.S. states by Amish population").[[170]](#cite_note-179)

The study found 16% are affiliated with [no religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion "Irreligion").[[171]](#cite_note-180)

Indiana is home to the [Benedictine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Saint_Benedict "Order of Saint Benedict") [St. Meinrad Archabbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Meinrad_Archabbey "St. Meinrad Archabbey"), one of two Catholic [archabbeys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archabbey "Archabbey") in the United States and 11 in the world. The [Lutheran Church–Missouri Synod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheran_Church–Missouri_Synod "Lutheran Church–Missouri Synod") has one of its two seminaries in [Fort Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concordia_Theological_Seminary "Concordia Theological Seminary"). Two evangelical Methodist denominations, the [Free Methodist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Methodist_Church "Free Methodist Church") and the [Wesleyan Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesleyan_Church "Wesleyan Church"), are headquartered in Indianapolis, as is the [Christian Church (Disciples of Christ)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Church_(Disciples_of_Christ) "Christian Church (Disciples of Christ)").[[172]](#cite_note-181)[[173]](#cite_note-182)

The [Fellowship of Grace Brethren Churches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fellowship_of_Grace_Brethren_Churches "Fellowship of Grace Brethren Churches") maintains offices and publishing work in [Winona Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winona_Lake,_Indiana "Winona Lake, Indiana").[[174]](#cite_note-183) [Huntington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huntington,_Indiana "Huntington, Indiana") serves as the home to the [Church of the United Brethren in Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_United_Brethren_in_Christ "Church of the United Brethren in Christ").[[175]](#cite_note-184) [Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anderson,_Indiana "Anderson, Indiana") is home to the headquarters of the [Church of God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_God_(Anderson,_Indiana) "Church of God (Anderson, Indiana)").[[176]](#cite_note-185) The headquarters of the [Missionary Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missionary_Church "Missionary Church") is in Fort Wayne.[[177]](#cite_note-186)

The [Friends United Meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friends_United_Meeting "Friends United Meeting") of the [Religious Society of Friends](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_Society_of_Friends "Religious Society of Friends"), the largest branch of American Quakerism, is based in [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Indiana "Richmond, Indiana"),[[178]](#cite_note-187) which also houses the oldest Quaker seminary in the United States, the [Earlham School of Religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earlham_School_of_Religion "Earlham School of Religion").[[179]](#cite_note-188) The [Islamic Society of North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Society_of_North_America "Islamic Society of North America") is headquartered in [Plainfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plainfield,_Indiana "Plainfield, Indiana").[[180]](#cite_note-189)
